{"parentUuid":"cd88c3b4-b31f-423f-8520-89bb0bbde984","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"08d5e347-ff76-44a1-a6b9-2efcb87ba3ce","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01DJZy1EeMNad8B6qExqNWfA","type":"tool_result","content":"T04: `cms deploy` and the site live on Pages\n## Parent\n\nPart of #1\n\n## What to build\n\nThe Admin runs `cms deploy`, confirms, and the rendered site is pushed to GitHub Pages, so a Published Post is Live. Deploy stays a separate command from `cms publish`. The README documents the workflow and the handover.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `cms deploy` is separate from `cms publish`. If the static site does not exist yet, it says to run `cms publish` first.\n- [ ] `cms deploy` asks for confirmation (default no). Answering no pushes nothing and exits cleanly. Answering yes pushes.\n- [ ] Only the static site is pushed. The database, `.env` and secrets are not included.\n- [ ] A successful Deploy records when it happened. A declined or failed one does not.\n- [ ] A Published Post is reachable at the GitHub Pages URL over HTTPS, and the `gh-pages` branch has no root-absolute paths.\n- [ ] Tests stub the push, so no test touches `gh-pages`.\n- [ ] The README describes publish-then-deploy and has a handover checklist: the repository, the database and the image folder, with the successor promoted before the outgoing Admin is Deactivated.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- T03 (#4)\n\n======\n## Problem Statement\n\nThe Chi Chapter of Delta Tau Delta at Kenyon College has an outdated website run by one alum who never updates it. Alumni cannot find updates, information is spread out, and active Officers cannot change anything. Prospective members, alumni, parents and Kenyon staff want to learn about the chapter's members, news, community service and history.\n\nThe chapter president needs a site that the Officers themselves can keep current, with these things never happening:\n\n- A graduated Officer can still log in and post.\n- An Editor deletes or changes the chapter history Page, or another Officer's Post.\n- A half-written Draft goes public, such as a rush announcement with the wrong date.\n- Members' personal information ends up public.\n\nThe people running it are college students who are comfortable with computers but not technical, and their roles change hands about once a year.\n\n## Solution\n\nA small CMS in which the president (Admin) and the other Officers (Editors) write Posts and Pages in a local admin console. The Admin runs `cms publish` to render the Published content into a static site, then `cms deploy` to push it to GitHub Pages after confirming. Nothing is Live until the Admin has reviewed and deployed it.\n\nThe console is deliberately plain. It has a dashboard that leads with what is waiting for release, a content list with a large status badge and only the filters that matter, and an editor with a prominent Preview. An Editor can only change their own Posts and the Pages the Admin has assigned to them. Officers who graduate are Deactivated immediately, and their past work and Byline stay.\n\nThe admin console never goes on the public internet (ADR-001). Editors work on a machine that runs the console, such as a shared chapter-house laptop.\n\n### The 7 required capabilities, for this client\n\n1. **Login with hashed passwords.** An Officer logs in with their email and password. Passwords are stored only as argon2 hashes. A wrong password gets a generic refusal that does not reveal whether the email exists. The Admin sets a temporary password for another User, who must change it at next login. The Admin's own recovery is a command on the machine.\n2. **Two roles, Admin and Editor.** The president is the Admin. Every other Officer is an Editor who writes their own Posts and edits only the Pages the Admin assigned to them. Access is enforced in code on every request, not by hiding links.\n3. **Posts.** Title, Slug, Markdown body, Draft or Published, author, and created, updated and published timestamps. Each Post shows a Byline with the author's full name, title and Term, fixed when the Post is first marked Published.\n4. **Pages in public Navigation.** Standing content such as About, History and Community Service has no author and no date. The Admin creates Pages, assigns one Editor to each, and chooses which appear in Navigation and in what order. One Page is the Home page.\n5. **Admin console.** A dashboard, a filterable content list, and an editor with Markdown Preview.\n6. **Admin-only user management with Deactivation.** The Admin creates Users, sets a role, title and Term, resets passwords, and Deactivates Users when they leave office. Deactivated Users are never deleted and cannot log in, even with an old session.\n7. **Static public site via `cms publish`.** `cms publish` renders only Published content into the static site, with sanitized Markdown and relative paths only. It shows what is new, changed or removed, and refuses if any Draft would appear. `cms deploy` is a separate command that confirms with the Admin before pushing to GitHub Pages.\n\n## User Stories\n\n### Login and accounts\n\n1. As an Officer, I want to log in with my email and password, so that I can reach the console without a separate username.\n2. As an Officer, I want to log out, so that the next person on a shared chapter laptop cannot use my account.\n3. As an Officer who typed a wrong password, I want a clear refusal that does not say whether my email exists, so that nobody can use the login form to find out who has an account.\n4. As an Officer who was just given a temporary password, I want to be made to choose my own at first login, so that the Admin never knows my real password.\n5. As an Officer choosing a password, I want to be told the minimum length, so that I do not guess at hidden rules.\n6. As the Admin, I want a session to end after 8 hours, so that a logged-in shared laptop does not stay open overnight.\n7. As the Admin, I want passwords stored only as argon2 hashes and never logged, so that a copied database does not expose anyone's password.\n8. As the Admin who forgot my own password, I want a command on the machine to reset it, so that I am not locked out when no email server exists.\n9. As the Admin setting up the CMS for the first time, I want `cms create-admin` to ask for my email and a hidden password, so that there is no default password in the code or the demo data.\n10. As an Admin, I want an anonymous visitor to be redirected to login on every admin URL, so that no admin content is ever shown to someone who is not logged in.\n\n### Roles and permissions\n\n11. As an Editor, I want to create Posts and edit only my own, so that I can do my job without being able to damage another Officer's work.\n12. As an Editor, I want to see every Post and Page in the content list but have other people's rows read-only, so that I know what is already written without being able to change it.\n13. As an Editor, I want to edit a Page the Admin assigned to me, so that I can keep my area (for example Community Service or History) current without needing the president.\n14. As an Editor, I want other Editors to be unable to edit my Assigned Page, so that my work is not overwritten by someone else.\n15. As an Editor, I want to mark my own Posts and my Assigned Pages Published, so that I am not waiting on the president for every step.\n16. As an Editor, I want to set my own Post back to Draft, so that I can take something down that I got wrong.\n17. As an Editor, I want to delete my own Drafts, so that I can clean up abandoned writing.\n18. As an Editor who tries to reach a page I am not allowed to use, I want a short message naming the current Admin and how to reach them, so that I know who to ask for higher permission.\n19. As an Editor, I want controls I cannot use to be hidden, so that the console is not cluttered with buttons that do nothing.\n20. As the Admin, I want an Editor who requests an admin-only URL directly to be refused (403 or redirect), so that hiding links is never the only protection.\n21. As the Admin, I want to edit and delete any Post or Page, so that I can correct problems when an Officer is unavailable.\n22. As the Admin, I want a deleted-Post rule that lets Editors delete only Drafts, so that a published announcement cannot be erased in one click.\n\n### Posts\n\n23. As an Editor, I want to write a Post with a title and a Markdown body, so that I can post news without knowing HTML.\n24. As an Editor, I want the Slug to be filled in from the title and editable until the Post is first Published, so that URLs are readable and I can fix awkward ones.\n25. As an Editor, I want a clear error if my Slug is already used by another Post, so that two Posts never share a URL.\n26. As a reader following a shared link, I want a Post's URL to stay the same after it is Published, so that links from alumni emails and social media do not break.\n27. As the Admin, I want to unlock a Slug after first publish when a correction is needed, so that the lock protects links but is not permanent.\n28. As an Editor, I want the Post to record who wrote it and when it was created, updated and published, so that readers and Officers can see its history.\n29. As a reader, I want each Post to show its author's full name, title and Term, so that I know who at the chapter is speaking and in which year.\n30. As an Editor, I want the Byline to be fixed when the Post is first Published, so that old Posts do not change when someone's title or Term later changes.\n31. As an Editor, I want to edit a Published Post in place and have the change reach the site at the next release, so that I can fix a typo without taking the Post down first.\n32. As an Editor, I want Posts that changed since the last Deploy to be marked in the content list, so that I can see which edits are not yet Live.\n\n### Pages and Navigation\n\n33. As the Admin, I want to create a Page and assign it to one Editor, so that every standing area of the site has a named maintainer.\n34. As the Admin, I want to choose which Pages appear in Navigation and their order, so that visitors see About, History and Community Service in a sensible order.\n35. As a visitor, I want the top of every public page to show Home, the chapter's Pages, and News, so that I can find things from anywhere.\n36. As a visitor, I want a Published Page without a Navigation entry to still be reachable by its URL, so that unlisted pages such as a rush information page can be shared directly.\n37. As the Admin, I want one Page flagged as the Home page, so that the site's front door is under my control.\n38. As the Admin, I want only the Admin to be able to delete a Page, so that an Editor cannot remove the chapter history Page.\n39. As the Admin, I want Pages to have no author and no date on the public site, so that standing content reads as the chapter's own voice.\n40. As the Admin, I want Pages whose Assigned editor was Deactivated to be left unassigned and Admin-only, so that nobody inherits access by accident.\n\n### Admin console\n\n41. As an Officer, I want a dashboard that leads with Pending release, so that I see first what is waiting to go public.\n42. As an Officer, I want the dashboard to show Drafts waiting and my own recent items, so that I can pick up where I left off.\n43. As the Admin, I want the dashboard to show Pages needing a new Assigned editor, so that turnover problems are visible.\n44. As an Officer, I want a content list with a large, clearly coloured status badge for Draft and Published, so that I can tell the status at a glance.\n45. As an Officer, I want to filter the content list by status, by type (Post or Page) and by author, so that I can find things quickly.\n46. As an Officer, I want a \"Mine\" shortcut on the content list, so that I can see only my own work in one click.\n47. As an Officer, I want the content list to have no categories, tags or bulk actions, so that it makes sense without a technical background.\n48. As an Editor, I want a Preview button right next to Save, so that I do not have to hunt for it.\n49. As an Editor, I want Preview to render the exact sanitized HTML that the Publish run would write, with the real Byline and Navigation, so that what I check is what visitors will see.\n50. As an Editor, I want Preview to say clearly that it is a draft preview and not public, so that I never confuse it with the live site.\n51. As an Editor, I want to see Markdown I typed with a script or event handler stripped in Preview, so that I find out about unsafe content before it is published.\n\n### User management\n\n52. As the Admin, I want to create a User with an email, display name, title, Term and role, so that a new Officer can start work.\n53. As the Admin, I want to change a User's role, title and Term, so that the console follows who currently holds each position.\n54. As the Admin, I want to Deactivate a User when they leave office, so that a graduated Officer can no longer log in or post.\n55. As the Admin, I want Deactivation to take effect immediately, including for sessions already open, so that there is no window in which a graduated Officer can post.\n56. As the Admin, I want Deactivated Users' Posts and Bylines to remain, so that chapter history is not rewritten when people graduate.\n57. As the Admin, I want to be unable to Deactivate or demote the last active Admin, so that the chapter is never locked out of its own site.\n58. As the Admin handing over, I want to promote my successor before Deactivating myself, so that the handover cannot leave the site without an Admin.\n59. As the Admin, I want the user list to warn me when Pages lose their Assigned editor, so that I reassign them during turnover.\n60. As the Admin, I want to reset another User's password to a temporary one, so that a forgetful Officer can recover without an email server.\n61. As an Editor, I want user management hidden and refused when I try its URLs directly, so that I cannot see or change anyone's account.\n62. As the Admin, I want a setting for the contact line shown to blocked Editors, so that the name and email they are told to contact stay current across turnover.\n\n### Markdown and safety\n\n63. As an Editor, I want basic formatting (headings, bold, italics, lists, links, quotes) to work, so that Posts are readable.\n64. As the Admin, I want all raw HTML in Markdown stripped, so that nobody can embed scripts or markup that is not part of the chapter's design.\n65. As a visitor, I want links that lead off the site to open safely, so that outside pages cannot reach back into the chapter's site.\n66. As a visitor, I want every internal link and file on the public site to use relative paths, so that the site works from the GitHub Pages project URL.\n67. As the Admin, I want all state-changing forms to carry a CSRF token and refuse requests without it, so that another website cannot make my browser change content.\n68. As the Admin, I want Users' emails and login names kept out of the public site, so that members' personal information is never published by the CMS.\n\n### Publishing and Deploy\n\n69. As the Admin, I want `cms publish` to render only Published Posts and Pages into the static site, so that drafts never leave the database.\n70. As the Admin, I want `cms publish` to refuse and explain when any Draft would appear in the output, so that a half-written rush announcement cannot slip through.\n71. As the Admin, I want `cms publish` to show what is new, changed and removed, so that I can review a release before it goes Live.\n72. As the Admin, I want `cms publish` to render Navigation, Home, News and each Post's Byline, so that the generated site is complete and I only run one command.\n73. As the Admin, I want the News page to list Posts newest-first by published date, so that alumni see the latest chapter news first.\n74. As the Admin, I want Home to show the Home Page followed by the 5 latest Posts, so that visitors see current activity straight away.\n75. As the Admin, I want `cms deploy` to ask me to confirm before it pushes, so that I give final approval to everything that becomes Live.\n76. As the Admin, I want `cms deploy` to push only the rendered static site, so that the database, `.env` and secrets are never published.\n77. As the Admin, I want unpublished (set back to Draft) content to disappear from the site at the next release, so that taking something down is possible.\n78. As a visitor (prospective member, alumni, parent or Kenyon staff), I want to read the chapter's news, community service and history without logging in, so that I can learn about the chapter.\n\n### Demo and handover\n\n79. As a grader or a new Admin, I want the demo seed script to build a demo database with an Admin, an Editor and a mix of Draft and Published content, so that I can use the CMS from a fresh clone with no manual steps.\n80. As the outgoing president, I want a handover checklist in the README, so that my successor knows what to take over (the repository, the database and the image folder) and what to do first.\n\n### Cuttable extras (built last)\n\n81. As the Admin, I want Markdown images from an admin-managed folder to appear in Posts and Pages by relative path, so that the chapter's photos can illustrate its history and service.\n82. As the Admin, I want only images referenced by Published content to be copied into the static site, so that an unapproved photo never goes public.\n83. As an Editor, I want an optional event date on a Post, so that rush and philanthropy events show when they happen.\n84. As a visitor, I want a Post with an event date to show both its published date and \"Event: <date>\", so that I can tell when something was posted from when it happens.\n85. As the Admin, I want `cms backup` to write a dated copy of the database outside the repository, so that one dead laptop does not erase the chapter's content.\n86. As the Admin, I want a username lock for 10 minutes after 5 failed logins, so that password guessing is slow. The lock is keyed on the email.\n87. As the Admin, I want `cms deploy` to warn me and stop if content has changed since the last `cms publish`, so that stale files in the static site are not pushed by accident.\n88. As the Admin, I want the `cms publish` summary to compare against what was last Deployed, so that \"changed\" means changed since the public site was updated.\n\n## Implementation Decisions\n\n**Architecture (fixed by ADR-001 and ADR-002):** a FastAPI admin console with Jinja templates and SQLite runs locally. `cms publish` writes a static site, and `cms deploy` pushes it to the `gh-pages` branch. There is no frontend framework and no build step. This spec adds no dependency. Asking the user is required before adding one.\n\n**Modules**\n- **Users and authentication:** the User record (email, display name, title, Term, role, active flag, password hash, must-change-password flag), argon2 hashing, signed session cookies with an 8-hour lifetime, and a per-request check that rejects Deactivated Users, so old sessions stop working. Login is by email. The wrong-password message is the same whether or not the email exists.\n- **Authorization:** one place that decides \"may this User do this to this item\". Every admin route calls it on the server side. The rules:\n  - Admin may do everything.\n  - An Editor may create Posts, may edit only their own Posts, and may edit only the Pages assigned to them.\n  - Editors may set their own Posts and Assigned Pages to Published, and may set their own Posts back to Draft.\n  - Editors may delete only their own Draft Posts.\n  - Only the Admin may create or delete Pages, assign editors, set Navigation, unlock a Slug, manage Users, or reset passwords.\n  - Everything else read-only for Editors.\n- **CSRF protection:** every state-changing form carries a token, and requests without it are rejected.\n- **Content:** Posts and Pages. A Post has a title, a unique Slug, a Markdown body, status, author, created, updated and published timestamps, and a Byline snapshot (name, title, Term) taken at first Published. A Page has a title, a unique Slug, a Markdown body, status, an Assigned editor, `show_in_nav`, `nav_order`, and an `is_home` flag that is true for exactly one Page. Slugs are generated from the title, lowercase with hyphens, unique within their kind, and locked after first Published except by the Admin. Editing a Published item changes it in place.\n- **Markdown rendering:** a single function that converts Markdown to HTML and then sanitizes it, used by both Preview and the Publish run so they cannot disagree. Raw HTML is stripped. Basic formatting and links are allowed, and outside links get `rel=\"noopener\"`.\n- **Admin console:** dashboard (Pending release first, then Drafts waiting, then my recent items, and for the Admin only Pages needing an editor), content list (status, type and author filters, \"Mine\" shortcut, large status badge, read-only rows for other people's items), editor with Preview (opens in a new tab with a \"draft preview, not public\" banner, refreshed on click), user management, and the blocked-Editor message that names the Admin and the contact line.\n- **Settings:** an Admin-editable contact line shown only inside the console.\n- **Publishing:** reads only Published content, renders pages, Posts, Home, News and Navigation into the static site using relative paths only, then verifies that no Draft appears in the output. Home is the Home Page followed by the 5 latest Posts as title, date and first paragraph. News lists every Post newest-first, with titles and dates, on one page.\n- **CLI:** `cms publish` renders, shows new, changed and removed items, and refuses on any Draft in the output. `cms deploy` is separate, asks for confirmation, then pushes. `cms create-admin <email>` takes a hidden password prompt. `cms reset-password <email>` resets the Admin's own password on the machine. Both take an email, not a username, to match login.\n- **Demo data:** the seed script creates the Admin and Editor from `.env` values (never hard-coded), plus sample Posts and Pages including at least one Draft and one Published item.\n\n**Passwords and sessions:** minimum 12 characters and no other rules. Temporary passwords force a change at next login.\n\n**Cuttable extras (built last, each independent of the core)**\n- **Images:** an admin-managed folder, referenced by relative path in Markdown. The Publish run copies only images referenced by Published content. Without this extra, image syntax is stripped by the sanitizer.\n- **Event date:** an optional field on Posts. A Post with one shows both its published date and \"Event: <date>\". News is still sorted by published date.\n- **`cms backup`:** writes a timestamped copy of the database outside the repository. Never committed.\n- **Login lockout:** keyed on the email, 5 failures locks it for 10 minutes. The message does not reveal whether the email exists.\n- **Deploy fingerprints:** a successful `cms deploy` records a fingerprint of each item, the baseline for \"changed since last Deploy\" in the `cms publish` summary and for the stale-site warning in `cms deploy`. Without this extra, the summary lists every Published item.\n\n## Testing Decisions\n\n**What makes a good test:** it exercises external behavior only, such as what a User sees or is refused, and what ends up in the generated files. It never reaches into private functions or table layouts. Each acceptance criterion in a ticket should have at least one test that clearly exercises it.\n\n**Two seams, both existing:**\n1. **The HTTP app through the test client** (`client` and the logged-in `client_as(\"admin\" | \"editor\")`). This covers login and logout, roles and permission refusals (including direct URL requests), Posts and Pages (Slug rules, the Draft and Published rules, Assigned editor, Byline snapshot), the dashboard, content list and filters, Preview, user management and Deactivation, and CSRF (replaying each POST with the token removed). Assertions are on status codes, redirects and response HTML.\n2. **Static output and the `cms` commands, against a temporary site folder and a temporary database.** `render_site` covers the content of the output: only Published content, no root-absolute paths, `<script>` and `onerror=` removed, Navigation order, Home and News, bylines with no email or login name. The `cms` command entry point covers `publish` refusing when a Draft would appear and `deploy` asking before pushing, with the actual push stubbed so nothing touches `gh-pages`.\n\n**Prior art:** The shared test fixtures already provides `client` and `client_as`, and the seeded users `admin@example.test` and `editor@example.test`. The T00 tests show the style for static output tests (publish into a temp folder, then read the files and check for relative paths). The T00 tests must keep passing.\n\n**Security checklist coverage:** each of the 10 items in the project's security checklist gets at least one test through the seams above, including the editor-cannot-reach-admin-routes and anonymous-is-redirected tests.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- Image upload by Editors (stretch goal; for now the Admin adds photos to a folder).\n- Remote access for Editors. The console stays local (ADR-001), and live admin deployment is a stretch goal.\n- Comments, search, or any server-side feature on the public site.\n- Categories, tags and bulk actions in the content list.\n- Revision history and a separate draft copy of a Live item.\n- A public member roster page or any automated listing of members.\n- Email, including emailed password resets.\n- Pagination of News, and live-as-you-type Preview.\n- A \"protected page\" flag. Page permissions come from the Assigned editor.\n- Position-based Page assignment. Pages are assigned to a User.\n- Any change to the stack in the project's dependency list.\n\n## Further Notes\n\n- **Build order:** the 7 required capabilities come first. The five cuttable extras (images, event date, `cms backup`, login lockout, deploy fingerprints) are the last tickets so they can be cut if time runs out. The core must work without them.\n- **Vocabulary:** use the terms in `CONTEXT.md`: Officer, User, Admin, Editor, Deactivated, Term, Byline, Post, Page, Assigned editor, Draft, Published, Live, Publish run, Deploy, Pending release, Navigation and Slug. \"Published\" means ready for release and \"Live\" means actually on the public site as of the latest Deploy.\n- **Personal information:** the CMS controls only what it renders from User records. Whatever an Editor types in a body is the Editor's responsibility, and Preview shows exactly what will be public.\n- **Repo is public:** photos added to the image folder are public the moment they are pushed, so unapproved photos stay out of the folder.\n- **Source material:** the client brief and WordPress field notes in the repository's notes folder, and the grill-with-docs session decisions.","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"a9992491-a5a9-499a-91cd-0e701102cd67","timestamp":"2026-10-06T22:28:47.927Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"T04: `cms deploy` and the site live on Pages\n## Parent\n\nPart of #1\n\n## What to build\n\nThe Admin runs `cms deploy`, confirms, and the rendered site is pushed to GitHub Pages, so a Published Post is Live. Deploy stays a separate command from `cms publish`. The README documents the workflow and the handover.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- [ ] `cms deploy` is separate from `cms publish`. If the static site does not exist yet, it says to run `cms publish` first.\n- [ ] `cms deploy` asks for confirmation (default no). Answering no pushes nothing and exits cleanly. Answering yes pushes.\n- [ ] Only the static site is pushed. The database, `.env` and secrets are not included.\n- [ ] A successful Deploy records when it happened. A declined or failed one does not.\n- [ ] A Published Post is reachable at the GitHub Pages URL over HTTPS, and the `gh-pages` branch has no root-absolute paths.\n- [ ] Tests stub the push, so no test touches `gh-pages`.\n- [ ] The README describes publish-then-deploy and has a handover checklist: the repository, the database and the image folder, with the successor promoted before the outgoing Admin is Deactivated.\n\n## Blocked by\n\n- T03 (#4)\n\n======\n## Problem Statement\n\nThe Chi Chapter of Delta Tau Delta at Kenyon College has an outdated website run by one alum who never updates it. Alumni cannot find updates, information is spread out, and active Officers cannot change anything. Prospective members, alumni, parents and Kenyon staff want to learn about the chapter's members, news, community service and history.\n\nThe chapter president needs a site that the Officers themselves can keep current, with these things never happening:\n\n- A graduated Officer can still log in and post.\n- An Editor deletes or changes the chapter history Page, or another Officer's Post.\n- A half-written Draft goes public, such as a rush announcement with the wrong date.\n- Members' personal information ends up public.\n\nThe people running it are college students who are comfortable with computers but not technical, and their roles change hands about once a year.\n\n## Solution\n\nA small CMS in which the president (Admin) and the other Officers (Editors) write Posts and Pages in a local admin console. The Admin runs `cms publish` to render the Published content into a static site, then `cms deploy` to push it to GitHub Pages after confirming. Nothing is Live until the Admin has reviewed and deployed it.\n\nThe console is deliberately plain. It has a dashboard that leads with what is waiting for release, a content list with a large status badge and only the filters that matter, and an editor with a prominent Preview. An Editor can only change their own Posts and the Pages the Admin has assigned to them. Officers who graduate are Deactivated immediately, and their past work and Byline stay.\n\nThe admin console never goes on the public internet (ADR-001). Editors work on a machine that runs the console, such as a shared chapter-house laptop.\n\n### The 7 required capabilities, for this client\n\n1. **Login with hashed passwords.** An Officer logs in with their email and password. Passwords are stored only as argon2 hashes. A wrong password gets a generic refusal that does not reveal whether the email exists. The Admin sets a temporary password for another User, who must change it at next login. The Admin's own recovery is a command on the machine.\n2. **Two roles, Admin and Editor.** The president is the Admin. Every other Officer is an Editor who writes their own Posts and edits only the Pages the Admin assigned to them. Access is enforced in code on every request, not by hiding links.\n3. **Posts.** Title, Slug, Markdown body, Draft or Published, author, and created, updated and published timestamps. Each Post shows a Byline with the author's full name, title and Term, fixed when the Post is first marked Published.\n4. **Pages in public Navigation.** Standing content such as About, History and Community Service has no author and no date. The Admin creates Pages, assigns one Editor to each, and chooses which appear in Navigation and in what order. One Page is the Home page.\n5. **Admin console.** A dashboard, a filterable content list, and an editor with Markdown Preview.\n6. **Admin-only user management with Deactivation.** The Admin creates Users, sets a role, title and Term, resets passwords, and Deactivates Users when they leave office. Deactivated Users are never deleted and cannot log in, even with an old session.\n7. **Static public site via `cms publish`.** `cms publish` renders only Published content into the static site, with sanitized Markdown and relative paths only. It shows what is new, changed or removed, and refuses if any Draft would appear. `cms deploy` is a separate command that confirms with the Admin before pushing to GitHub Pages.\n\n## User Stories\n\n### Login and accounts\n\n1. As an Officer, I want to log in with my email and password, so that I can reach the console without a separate username.\n2. As an Officer, I want to log out, so that the next person on a shared chapter laptop cannot use my account.\n3. As an Officer who typed a wrong password, I want a clear refusal that does not say whether my email exists, so that nobody can use the login form to find out who has an account.\n4. As an Officer who was just given a temporary password, I want to be made to choose my own at first login, so that the Admin never knows my real password.\n5. As an Officer choosing a password, I want to be told the minimum length, so that I do not guess at hidden rules.\n6. As the Admin, I want a session to end after 8 hours, so that a logged-in shared laptop does not stay open overnight.\n7. As the Admin, I want passwords stored only as argon2 hashes and never logged, so that a copied database does not expose anyone's password.\n8. As the Admin who forgot my own password, I want a command on the machine to reset it, so that I am not locked out when no email server exists.\n9. As the Admin setting up the CMS for the first time, I want `cms create-admin` to ask for my email and a hidden password, so that there is no default password in the code or the demo data.\n10. As an Admin, I want an anonymous visitor to be redirected to login on every admin URL, so that no admin content is ever shown to someone who is not logged in.\n\n### Roles and permissions\n\n11. As an Officer, I want the content list to have no categories, tags or bulk actions, so that it makes sense without a technical background.\n48. As an Editor, I want a Preview button right next to Save, so that I do not have to hunt for it.\n49. As an Editor, I want Preview to render the exact sanitized HTML that the Publish run would write, with the real Byline and Navigation, so that what I check is what visitors will see.\n50. As an Editor, I want Preview to say clearly that it is a draft preview and not public, so that I never confuse it with the live site.\n51. As an Editor, I want to see Markdown I typed with a script or event handler stripped in Preview, so that I find out about unsafe content before it is published.\n\n### User management\n\n52. As the Admin, I want to create a User with an email, display name, title, Term and role, so that a new Officer can start work.\n53. As the Admin, I want to change a User's role, title and Term, so that the console follows who currently holds each position.\n54. As the Admin, I want to Deactivate a User when they leave office, so that a graduated Officer can no longer log in or post.\n55. As the Admin, I want Deactivation to take effect immediately, including for sessions already open, so that there is no window in which a graduated Officer can post.\n56. As the Admin, I want Deactivated Users' Posts and Bylines to remain, so that chapter history is not rewritten when people graduate.\n57. As the Admin, I want to be unable to Deactivate or demote the last active Admin, so that the chapter is never locked out of its own site.\n58. As the Admin handing over, I want to promote my successor before Deactivating myself, so that the handover cannot leave the site without an Admin.\n59. As the Admin, I want the user list to warn me when Pages lose their Assigned editor, so that I reassign them during turnover.\n60. As the Admin, I want to reset another User's password to a temporary one, so that a forgetful Officer can recover without an email server.\n61. As an Editor, I want user management hidden and refused when I try its URLs directly, so that I cannot see or change anyone's account.\n62. As the Admin, I want a setting for the contact line shown to blocked Editors, so that the name and email they are told to contact stay current across turnover.\n\n### Markdown and safety\n\n63. As an Editor, I want basic formatting (headings, bold, italics, lists, links, quotes) to work, so that Posts are readable.\n64. As the Admin, I want all raw HTML in Markdown stripped, so that nobody can embed scripts or markup that is not part of the chapter's design.\n65. As a visitor, I want links that lead off the site to open safely, so that outside pages cannot reach back into the chapter's site.\n66. As a visitor, I want every internal link and file on the public site to use relative paths, so that the site works from the GitHub Pages project URL.\n67. As the Admin, I want all state-changing forms to carry a CSRF token and refuse requests without it, so that another website cannot make my browser change content.\n68. As the Admin, I want Users' emails and login names kept out of the public site, so that members' personal information is never published by the CMS.\n\n### Publishing and Deploy\n\n69. As the Admin, I want `cms publish` to render only Published Posts and Pages into the static site, so that drafts never leave the database.\n70. As the Admin, I want `cms publish` to refuse and explain when any Draft would appear in the output, so that a half-written rush announcement cannot slip through.\n71. As the Admin, I want `cms publish` to show what is new, changed and removed, so that I can review a release before it goes Live.\n72. As the Admin, I want `cms publish` to render Navigation, Home, News and each Post's Byline, so that the generated site is complete and I only run one command.\n73. As the Admin, I want the News page to list Posts newest-first by published date, so that alumni see the latest chapter news first.\n74. As the Admin, I want Home to show the Home Page followed by the 5 latest Posts, so that visitors see current activity straight away.\n75. As the Admin, I want `cms deploy` to ask me to confirm before it pushes, so that I give final approval to everything that becomes Live.\n76. As the Admin, I want `cms deploy` to push only the rendered static site, so that the database, `.env` and secrets are never published.\n77. As the Admin, I want unpublished (set back to Draft) content to disappear from the site at the next release, so that taking something down is possible.\n78. As a visitor (prospective member, alumni, parent or Kenyon staff), I want to read the chapter's news, community service and history without logging in, so that I can learn about the chapter.\n\n### Demo and handover\n\n79. As a grader or a new Admin, I want the demo seed script to build a demo database with an Admin, an Editor and a mix of Draft and Published content, so that I can use the CMS from a fresh clone with no manual steps.\n80. As the outgoing president, I want a handover checklist in the README, so that my successor knows what to take over (the repository, the database and the image folder) and what to do first.\n\n### Cuttable extras (built last)\n\n81. As the Admin, I want Markdown images from an admin-managed folder to appear in Posts and Pages by relative path, so that the chapter's photos can illustrate its history and service.\n82. As the Admin, I want only images referenced by Published content to be copied into the static site, so that an unapproved photo never goes public.\n83. As an Editor, I want an optional event date on a Post, so that rush and philanthropy events show when they happen.\n84. As a visitor, I want a Post with an event date to show both its published date and \"Event: <date>\", so that I can tell when something was posted from when it happens.\n85. As the Admin, I want `cms backup` to write a dated copy of the database outside the repository, so that one dead laptop does not erase the chapter's content.\n86. As the Admin, I want a username lock for 10 minutes after 5 failed logins, so that password guessing is slow. The lock is keyed on the email.\n87. As the Admin, I want `cms deploy` to warn me and stop if content has changed since the last `cms publish`, so that stale files in the static site are not pushed by accident.\n88. As the Admin, I want the `cms publish` summary to compare against what was last Deployed, so that \"changed\" means changed since the public site was updated.\n\n## Implementation Decisions\n\n**Architecture (fixed by ADR-001 and ADR-002):** a FastAPI admin console with Jinja templates and SQLite runs locally. `cms publish` writes a static site, and `cms deploy` pushes it to the `gh-pages` branch. There is no frontend framework and no build step. This spec adds no dependency. Asking the user is required before adding one.\n\n**Modules**\n- **Users and authentication:** the User record (email, display name, title, Term, role, active flag, password hash, must-change-password flag), argon2 hashing, signed session cookies with an 8-hour lifetime, and a per-request check that rejects Deactivated Users, so old sessions stop working. Login is by email. The wrong-password message is the same whether or not the email exists.\n- **Authorization:** one place that decides \"may this User do this to this item\". Every admin route calls it on the server side. The rules:\n  - Admin may do everything.\n  - An Editor may create Posts, may edit only their own Posts, and may edit only the Pages assigned to them.\n  - Editors may set their own Posts and Assigned Pages to Published, and may set their own Posts back to Draft.\n  - Editors may delete only their own Draft Posts.\n  - Only the Admin may create or delete Pages, assign editors, set Navigation, unlock a Slug, manage Users, or reset passwords.\n  - Everything else read-only for Editors.\n- **CSRF protection:** every state-changing form carries a token, and requests without it are rejected.\n- **Content:** Posts and Pages. A Post has a title, a unique Slug, a Markdown body, status, author, created, updated and published timestamps, and a Byline snapshot (name, title, Term) taken at first Published. A Page has a title, a unique Slug, a Markdown body, status, an Assigned editor, `show_in_nav`, `nav_order`, and an `is_home` flag that is true for exactly one Page. Slugs are generated from the title, lowercase with hyphens, unique within their kind, and locked after first Published except by the Admin. Editing a Published item changes it in place.\n- **Markdown rendering:** a single function that converts Markdown to HTML and then sanitizes it, used by both Preview and the Publish run so they cannot disagree. Raw HTML is stripped. Basic formatting and links are allowed, and outside links get `rel=\"noopener\"`.\n- **Admin console:** dashboard (Pending release first, then Drafts waiting, then my recent items, and for the Admin only Pages needing an editor), content list (status, type and author filters, \"Mine\" shortcut, large status badge, read-only rows for other people's items), editor with Preview (opens in a new tab with a \"draft preview, not public\" banner, refreshed on click), user management, and the blocked-Editor message that names the Admin and the contact line.\n- **Settings:** an Admin-editable contact line shown only inside the console.\n- **Publishing:** reads only Published content, renders pages, Posts, Home, News and Navigation into the static site using relative paths only, then verifies that no Draft appears in the output. Home is the Home Page followed by the 5 latest Posts as title, date and first paragraph. News lists every Post newest-first, with titles and dates, on one page.\n- **CLI:** `cms publish` renders, shows new, changed and removed items, and refuses on any Draft in the output. `cms deploy` is separate, asks for confirmation, then pushes. `cms create-admin <email>` takes a hidden password prompt. `cms reset-password <email>` resets the Admin's own password on the machine. Both take an email, not a username, to match login.\n- **Demo data:** the seed script creates the Admin and Editor from `.env` values (never hard-coded), plus sample Posts and Pages including at least one Draft and one Published item.\n\n**Passwords and sessions:** minimum 12 characters and no other rules. Temporary passwords force a change at next login.\n\n**Cuttable extras (built last, each independent of the core)**\n- **Images:** an admin-managed folder, referenced by relative path in Markdown. The Publish run copies only images referenced by Published content. Without this extra, image syntax is stripped by the sanitizer.\n- **Event date:** an optional field on Posts. A Post with one shows both its published date and \"Event: <date>\". News is still sorted by published date.\n- **`cms backup`:** writes a timestamped copy of the database outside the repository. Never committed.\n- **Login lockout:** keyed on the email, 5 failures locks it for 10 minutes. The message does not reveal whether the email exists.\n- **Deploy fingerprints:** a successful `cms deploy` records a fingerprint of each item, the baseline for \"changed since last Deploy\" in the `cms publish` summary and for the stale-site warning in `cms deploy`. Without this extra, the summary lists every Published item.\n\n## Testing Decisions\n\n**What makes a good test:** it exercises external behavior only, such as what a User sees or is refused, and what ends up in the generated files. It never reaches into private functions or table layouts. Each acceptance criterion in a ticket should have at least one test that clearly exercises it.\n\n**Two seams, both existing:**\n1. **The HTTP app through the test client** (`client` and the logged-in `client_as(\"admin\" | \"editor\")`). This covers login and logout, roles and permission refusals (including direct URL requests), Posts and Pages (Slug rules, the Draft and Published rules, Assigned editor, Byline snapshot), the dashboard, content list and filters, Preview, user management and Deactivation, and CSRF (replaying each POST with the token removed). Assertions are on status codes, redirects and response HTML.\n2. **Static output and the `cms` commands, against a temporary site folder and a temporary database.** `render_site` covers the content of the output: only Published content, no root-absolute paths, `<script>` and `onerror=` removed, Navigation order, Home and News, bylines with no email or login name. The `cms` command entry point covers `publish` refusing when a Draft would appear and `deploy` asking before pushing, with the actual push stubbed so nothing touches `gh-pages`.\n\n**Prior art:** The shared test fixtures already provides `client` and `client_as`, and the seeded users `admin@example.test` and `editor@example.test`. The T00 tests show the style for static output tests (publish into a temp folder, then read the files and check for relative paths). The T00 tests must keep passing.\n\n**Security checklist coverage:** each of the 10 items in the project's security checklist gets at least one test through the seams above, including the editor-cannot-reach-admin-routes and anonymous-is-redirected tests.\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- Image upload by Editors (stretch goal; for now the Admin adds photos to a folder).\n- Remote access for Editors. The console stays local (ADR-001), and live admin deployment is a stretch goal.\n- Comments, search, or any server-side feature on the public site.\n- Categories, tags and bulk actions in the content list.\n- Revision history and a separate draft copy of a Live item.\n- A public member roster page or any automated listing of members.\n- Email, including emailed password resets.\n- Pagination of News, and live-as-you-type Preview.\n- A \"protected page\" flag. Page permissions come from the Assigned editor.\n- Position-based Page assignment. Pages are assigned to a User.\n- Any change to the stack in the project's dependency list.\n\n## Further Notes\n\n- **Build order:** the 7 required capabilities come first. The five cuttable extras (images, event date, `cms backup`, login lockout, deploy fingerprints) are the last tickets so they can be cut if time runs out. The core must work without them.\n- **Vocabulary:** use the terms in `CONTEXT.md`: Officer, User, Admin, Editor, Deactivated, Term, Byline, Post, Page, Assigned editor, Draft, Published, Live, Publish run, Deploy, Pending release, Navigation and Slug. \"Published\" means ready for release and \"Live\" means actually on the public site as of the latest Deploy.\n- **Personal information:** the CMS controls only what it renders from User records. Whatever an Editor types in a body is the Editor's responsibility, and Preview shows exactly what will be public.\n- **Repo is public:** photos added to the image folder are public the moment they are pushed, so unapproved photos stay out of the folder.\n- **Source material:** the client brief and WordPress field notes in the repository's notes folder, and the grill-with-docs session decisions.","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"serverClassifierContext":{"request":"764e9e96-4a45-4b36-9647-0d4ab1385646","context":{"git_state":{"cwd":"/home/faisa/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","root":"/home/faisa/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","branch":"main","default_branch":"main","status":{"clean":false,"counts":{"staged":0,"modified":0,"untracked":null,"untracked_normal":2},"porcelain":null,"truncated":false},"visibility":{"origin":{"host":"github.com","remote":"faisalsyed922-cloud/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"},"push_remote":"origin","remotes":[{"name":"origin","host":"github.com","remote":"faisalsyed922-cloud/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"}],"visibility_cache":[{"host":"github.com","remote":"faisalsyed922-cloud/iphs400-mp2-cms","visibility":"public"}]}},"live_cwd":"/home/faisa/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","platform":"wsl"}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"cd88c3b4-b31f-423f-8520-89bb0bbde984","session_id":"4b1a37e3-ea11-48e9-baa9-b66b752b7a0d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/home/faisa/code/iphs400-mp2-cms","sessionId":"c33cf7b9-7a5f-41e8-bb05-4583ba594c98","version":"2.1.292","gitBranch":"main"}

= \"iphs400-mp2-cms\"\nversion = \"0.1.0\"\ndescription = \"IPHS 400 MP2 — a small web CMS with a local admin console and a static public site\"\nrequires-python = \">=3.12\"\ndependencies = [\n    \"fastapi>=0.115\",\n    \"uvicorn[standard]>=0.32\",\n    \"jinja2>=3.1\",\n    \"python-multipart>=0.0.12\",   # form posts\n    \"itsdangerous>=2.2\",          # signed session cookies\n    \"argon2-cffi>=23.1\",          # password hashing (never store plain passwords)\n    \"markdown-it-py>=3.0\",        # Markdown -> HTML\n    \"nh3>=0.2\",                   # HTML sanitizer (strips <script> etc.)\n    \"ghp-import>=2.1\",            # push site/ to the gh-pages branch\n]\n\n[dependency-groups]\ndev = [\"pytest>=8.3\", \"httpx>=0.28\"]\n\n[project.scripts]\ncms = \"app.cli:main\"\n\n[tool.pytest.ini_options]\ntestpaths = [\"tests\"]\n\n[build-system]\nrequires = [\"hatchling\"]\nbuild-backend = \"hatchling.build\"\n\n[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel]\npackages = [\"app\"]\n# IPHS 400 — Mini-Project #2 starter (Web CMS)\n\nClick **Use this template** → name your repo **`iphs400-mp2-cms`** → make it **Public**.\nDo not fork: a fork arrives without an Issues tab, and your tickets live in Issues.\n\n## Start here\n\n1. `docs/manual_iphs400_mp2-web-cms_20260922.md` — the manual. Read Part 0 and Part 1 first.\n2. `docs/mp2-grading-rubric_20260922.md` — how you are graded. Read it **before** you build.\n3. `docs/mp2-setup_context-threshold-hook_20260922.md` — Exercise A, in Part 4 of the manual.\n\n## Run it\n\n```bash\nuv sync\ncp .env.example .env\nuv run cms serve        # then open http://localhost:8000/admin  -> \"T00: hello admin\"\n```\n\n## What is here\n\n```text\n.claude/hooks/     the context meter and usage ledger (Exercise A lives in ctx_guard.py)\nscripts/           usage_report.py (Exercise B lives in spend()), check_submission.py, seed_demo.py\ntests/             the exercise tests, plus helpers such as client_as(\"editor\")\napp/, templates/   the T00 skeleton — every CMS feature is yours to build\ndocs/adr/          two example decision records\n```\n\nTwo functions are deliberately unfinished and their tests fail until you write them:\n`decide()` in `.claude/hooks/ctx_guard.py` and `spend()` in `scripts/usage_report.py`.\nBoth are graded. Use `/tdd`, as the manual says.\n\n## Deadlines\n\nStage 1 (`mp2-mvp` tag): Tue Sep 29, 2:40 pm Eastern (soft target).\nStage 2 (`mp2-final` tag): Tue Oct 6, 2:40 pm Eastern, grace until Wed Oct 7, 2:40 pm.\n\nRun `uv run python scripts/check_submission.py --stage 2` before you submit.\n\n## Generative AI Use Statement\n\n*(Required. Replace this section: name the models and skills you used, quote two\nprompts you really sent, describe one real model failure, and include a\n\"Backends used\" table if you ever switched providers.)*\norigin\thttps://github.com/faisalsyed922-cloud/iphs400-mp2-cms.git (fetch)\norigin\thttps://github.com/faisalsyed922-cloud/iphs400-mp2-cms.git (push)\n# ADR-001: The admin console runs locally; the public site is a static export\n\n**Status:** accepted (set by the assignment)\n**Date:** 2026-09-22\n\n## Context\n\nA CMS needs a server, a database, and login. GitHub Pages serves static files\nonly: it cannot run Python and has no database. Students have two weeks and no\nhosting budget, and a first web application with real authentication is an\nattractive target the moment it is public.\n\n## Decision\n\nThe admin console (FastAPI + SQLite) runs on the student's own machine. A\n`cms publish` step renders the published content to static HTML in `site/`, which\nis deployed to GitHub Pages on the `gh-pages` branch.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- Login, roles, and the database are never exposed to the internet.\n- The public site cannot have server-side features (search, comments) without a\n  different host; a stretch goal may add a live admin deploy behind a security gate.\n- Publishing is explicit: content changes are not live until `cms publish` runs,\n  which is a behaviour worth documenting for your client.\n- Relative paths become mandatory, because Pages serves the site from a subfolder.\n\n## Why this is an ADR\n\nIt is hard to reverse (it shapes every route, template, and deploy step) and it\nis surprising without context (most CMSes are servers). That is the bar; most\ndecisions do not meet it and belong in the spec or the glossary instead.\n# ADR-002: FastAPI + Jinja + SQLite, with no frontend framework\n\n**Status:** accepted (set by the assignment)\n**Date:** 2026-09-22\n\n## Context\n\nThe project has to be readable by someone who has never built a web application,\ngradable from a clean clone, and finishable in two weeks on a $20 plan. = [\"app\"]\n# IPHS 400 — Mini-Project #2 starter (Web CMS)\n\nClick **Use this template** → name your repo **`iphs400-mp2-cms`** → make it **Public**.\nDo not fork: a fork arrives without an Issues GitHub Pages serves static files\nonly: it cannot run Python and has no database. Students have two weeks and no\nhosting budget, and a first web application with real authentication is an\nattractive target the moment it is public.\n\n## Decision\n\nThe admin console (FastAPI + SQLite) runs on the student's own machine. A\n`cms publish` step renders the published content to static HTML in `site/`, which\nis deployed to GitHub Pages on the `gh-pages` branch.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- Login, roles, and the database are never exposed to the internet.\n- The public site cannot have server-side features (search, comments) without a\n  different host; a stretch goal may add a live admin deploy behind a security gate.\n- Publishing is explicit: content changes are not live until `cms publish` runs,\n  which is a behaviour worth documenting for your client.\n- Relative paths become mandatory, because Pages serves the site from a subfolder.\n\n## Why this is an ADR\n\nIt is hard to reverse (it shapes every route, template, and deploy step) and it\nis surprising without context (most CMSes are servers). {"parentUuid":"e60267a3-4482-4780-88c9-ebe27038f4b2","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"08d5e347-ff76-44a1-a6b9-2efcb87ba3ce","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014BLZBHPgfRdpmDxen1RfUV","type":"tool_result","content":"\"\"\"The `cms` command: serve, publish, deploy.\n\n    uv run cms serve      # admin console + public preview at http://localhost:8000\n    uv run cms publish    # render site/ from published content\n    uv run cms deploy     # push site/ to the gh-pages branch (Pages serves it)\n    uv run cms create-admin EMAIL   # make an Admin; asks for the password\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport getpass\nimport sqlite3\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\n\nfrom app import settings, users\nfrom app.publish import DraftInOutput, render\n\n\ndef main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog=\"cms\")\n    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest=\"command\", required=True)\n    serve = sub.add_parser(\"serve\", help=\"run the admin console locally\")\n    serve.add_argument(\"--port\", type=int, default=8000)\n    serve.add_argument(\"--reload\", action=\"store_true\", default=True)\n    sub.add_parser(\"publish\", help=\"render site/ from published content\")\n    deploy = sub.add_parser(\"deploy\", help=\"push site/ to gh-pages\")\n    deploy.add_argument(\"--message\", default=\"Publish site\")\n    create = sub.add_parser(\"create-admin\", help=\"create an Admin account\")\n    create.add_argument(\"email\")\n    create.add_argument(\"--name\", default=\"Admin\", help=\"display name\")\n    args = parser.parse_args(argv)\n\n    if args.command == \"create-admin\":\n        password = getpass.getpass(\"Password: \")\n        if password != getpass.getpass(\"Password again: \"):\n            print(\"The passwords did not match; nothing was created.\")\n            return 1\n        users.init_db(settings.DATABASE_PATH)\n        try:\n            users.create_user(settings.DATABASE_PATH, email=args.email,\n                              password=password, role=\"admin\",\n                              display_name=args.name)\n        except sqlite3.IntegrityError:\n            print(f\"{args.email} already has an account.\")\n            return 1\n        print(f\"Created Admin {args.email}.\")\n        return 0\n\n    if args.command == \"serve\":\n        if settings.SECRET_KEY == settings.DEV_SECRET_KEY:\n            print(\"CMS_SECRET_KEY is not set (copy .env.example to .env and \"\n                  \"change it). Refusing to serve with the public dev key.\")\n            return 1\n        import uvicorn\n\n        uvicorn.run(\"app.main:app\", port=args.port, reload=args.reload)\n        return 0\n\n    if args.command == \"publish\":\n        try:\n            out, items = render()\n        except DraftInOutput as exc:\n            print(exc)\n            return 1\n        print(\"Rendered:\")\n        for item in items:\n            print(f\"  - {item}\")\n        print(f\"Wrote {out}. Preview it with:  python3 -m http.server -d {out} 8001\")\n        return 0\n\n    if args.command == \"deploy\":\n        if not settings.SITE.exists():\n            print(\"site/ does not exist yet — run `uv run cms publish` first.\")\n            return 1\n        result = subprocess.run(\n            [sys.executable, \"-m\", \"ghp_import\", \"-n\", \"-p\", \"-m\", args.message,\n             str(settings.SITE)],\n        )\n        if result.returncode == 0:\n            print(\"Pushed to gh-pages. Settings -> Pages -> Deploy from a branch -> \"\n                  \"gh-pages / root, then wait up to 10 minutes.\")\n        return result.returncode\n    return 1\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    raise SystemExit(main())\n\"\"\"Render the public site into site/ as plain HTML.\n\nT00 publishes a placeholder home page. As you build content types, extend\nrender_site() to write one file per published item. Two rules the rubric checks:\n\n  1. Only PUBLISHED content is written here. A draft that reaches site/ is a bug.\n  2. Every href and src is RELATIVE (\"style.css\", \"posts/x.html\"), never\n     root-absolute (\"/style.css\"), because Pages serves this from a subfolder.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport re\nimport shutil\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom markupsafe import escape\nfrom jinja2 import Environment, FileSystemLoader, select_autoescape\n\nfrom app import settings\nfrom app.markdown import render_markdown\nfrom app.posts import format_byline\nfrom app.users import connect\n\nCSS = \"\"\"/* Minimal starter styles — make them yours. */\n:root { color-scheme: light dark; }\nbody { font: 16px/1.6 system-ui, sans-serif; margin: 0 auto; max-width: 42rem; padding: 1rem; }\nheader a { font-weight: 700; text-decoration: none; }\nmain { margin-block: 2rem; }\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef environment() -> Environment:\n    return Environment(\n        loader=FileSystemLoader(str(settings.TEMPLATES)),\n        autoescape=select_autoescape([\"html\"]),\n    )\n\n\nclass DraftInOutput(Exception):\n    \"\"\"A Draft's title or Slug would appear in the generated site.\"\"\"\n\n\ndef _posts_where(database: Path, where: str) -> list:\n    if not database.exists():\n        return []\n    with connect(database) as conn:\n        if not conn.execute(\"select 1 from sqlite_master where name='posts'\").fetchone():\n            return []\n        return conn.execute(\n            f\"select * from posts where {where} order by published_at desc, id desc\"\n        ).fetchall()\n\n\ndef _published_posts(database: Path) -> list:\n    return _posts_where(database, \"status='published'\")\n\n\ndef _draft_rows(database: Path) -> list:\n    return _posts_where(database, \"status!='published'\")\n\n\ndef _first_paragraph(html: str) -> str:\n    match = re.search(r\"<p>.*?</p>\", html, re.S)\n    return match.group(0) if match else \"\"\n\n\ndef _check_no_drafts(site: Path, database: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Refuse if any Draft made it into any page of the site.\n\n    Every post entry carries a data-post-id marker and links to\n    posts/<slug>.html. Both are compared with the Drafts straight from the\n    database (ids and Slugs are unique), on every file, so a Draft leaking onto\n    Home or News is caught as surely as one on its own page. Titles are not\n    matched: a Draft called \"News\" must not block every publish.\n    \"\"\"\n    drafts = _draft_rows(database)\n    for f in sorted(site.rglob(\"*\")):\n        if not f.is_file():\n            continue\n        text = f.read_text()\n        marked = {int(i) for i in re.findall(r'data-post-id=\"(\\d+)\"', text)}\n        for draft in drafts:\n            if (draft[\"id\"] in marked or f\"posts/{draft['slug']}.html\" in text\n                    or f.name == f\"{draft['slug']}.html\" and f.parent.name == \"posts\"):\n                raise DraftInOutput(\n                    f\"Refusing to publish: the Draft \\\"{draft['title']}\\\" would \"\n                    f\"appear in {f.relative_to(site)}. Drafts must never leave the \"\n                    \"database. The site was not written.\")\n\n\ndef render(out: Path | None = None, database: Path | None = None) -> tuple[Path, list[str]]:\n    \"\"\"Render Published content into `out`; return it and a list of rendered items.\n\n    Builds in a sibling folder and swaps it in only after the Draft check\n    passes, so a refusal never leaves a half-finished site.\n    \"\"\"\n    out = out or settings.SITE\n    database = database or settings.DATABASE_PATH\n    work = out.parent / (out.name + \".building\")\n    if work.exists():\n        shutil.rmtree(work)\n    work.mkdir(parents=True)\n    try:\n        items = _render_into(work, database)\n    except BaseException:\n        shutil.rmtree(work, ignore_errors=True)\n        raise\n    if out.exists():\n        shutil.rmtree(out)\n    work.rename(out)\n    return out, items\n\n\ndef _render_into(work: Path, database: Path) -> list[str]:\n    env = environment()\n    published = _published_posts(database)\n    entries = []\n    for post in published:\n        body = render_markdown(post[\"body\"])\n        entries.append({\n            \"id\": post[\"id\"], \"title\": post[\"title\"], \"slug\": post[\"slug\"], \"body\": body,\n            \"excerpt\": _first_paragraph(body),\n            \"date\": (post[\"published_at\"] or \"\")[:10],\n            \"byline\": format_byline(post[\"byline_name\"], post[\"byline_title\"],\n                                    post[\"byline_term\"]),\n            \"href\": f\"posts/{post['slug']}.html\",\n        })\n\n    (work / \"style.css\").write_text(CSS)\n    (work / \"posts\").mkdir()\n    nav = {\"home_path\": \"index.html\", \"news_path\": \"news.html\"}\n\n    def write(path: str, template: str, prefix: str, **ctx) -> None:\n        # prefix is \"\" for str:\napp/posts.py:138:def byline(post) -> str:\n---\nname: tdd\ndescription: Test-driven development. Use when the user wants to build features or fix bugs test-first, mentions \"red-green-refactor\", or wants integration tests.\n---\n\n# Test-Driven Development\n\nTDD is the red → green loop. This skill is the reference that makes that loop produce tests worth keeping: what a good test is, where tests go, the anti-patterns, and the rules of the loop. Every section applies on every cycle: consult them before and during the loop, not after.\n\nWhen exploring the codebase, read `GLOSSARY.md` (if it exists) so test names and interface vocabulary match the project's domain language, and respect ADRs in the area you're touching.\n\n## What a good test is\n\nTests verify behavior through public interfaces, not implementation details. Code can change entirely; tests shouldn't. A good test reads like a specification: \"user can checkout with valid cart\" tells you exactly what capability exists, and it survives refactors because it doesn't care about internal structure.\n\nSee [tests.md](tests.md) for examples and [mocking.md](mocking.md) for mocking guidelines.\n\n## Seams: where tests go\n\nA **seam** is the public boundary you test at: the interface where you observe behavior without reaching inside. Tests live at seams, never against internals.\n\n**Test only at pre-agreed seams.** Before writing any test, write down the seams under test and confirm them with the user. No test is written at an unconfirmed seam. You can't test everything, so agreeing the seams up front is how testing effort lands on the critical paths and complex logic instead of every edge case.\n\nAsk: \"What's the public interface, and which seams should we test?\"\n\nWhen the shape of that interface is itself in question (how deep the module is, where the seam belongs, what the interface should expose), call the Skill tool with \"codebase-design\" for the vocabulary. It is the shared source of the module, interface, depth, seam, adapter, leverage and locality terms, and it is a reference to consult, not a session to run.\n\n## Anti-patterns\n\n- **Implementation-coupled**: mocks internal collaborators, tests private methods, or verifies through a side channel (querying the database instead of using the interface). The tell: the test breaks when you refactor but behavior hasn't changed.\n- **Tautological**: the assertion recomputes the expected value the way the code does (`expect(add(a, b)).toBe(a + b)`, a snapshot derived by hand the same way, a constant asserted equal to itself), so it passes by construction and can never disagree with the code. Expected values must come from an independent source of truth: a known-good literal, a worked example, the spec.\n- **Horizontal slicing**: writing all tests first, then all implementation. Bulk tests verify _imagined_ behavior: you test the _shape_ of things rather than user-facing behavior, the tests go insensitive to real changes, and you commit to test structure before understanding the implementation. Work in **vertical slices** instead: one test → one implementation → repeat, each test a **tracer bullet** that responds to what the last cycle taught you.\n\n## Rules of the loop\n\n- **Red before green.** Write the failing test first, then only enough code to pass it. Don't anticipate future tests or add speculative features.\n- **One slice at a time.** One seam, one test, one minimal implementation per cycle.\n- **Refactoring is not part of the loop.** It belongs to the review stage (see the `code-review` skill), not the red → green implementation cycle.","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"9ee40124-7409-4c8a-9546-d21f942f3b72","timestamp":"2026-10-06T22:28:54.073Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"\"\"\"The `cms` command: serve, publish, deploy.\n\n    uv run cms serve      # admin console + public preview at http://localhost:8000\n    uv run cms publish    # render site/ from published content\n    uv run cms deploy     # push site/ to the gh-pages branch (Pages serves it)\n    uv run cms create-admin EMAIL   # make an Admin; asks for the password\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport getpass\nimport sqlite3\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\n\nfrom app import settings, users\nfrom app.publish import DraftInOutput, render\n\n\ndef main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog=\"cms\")\n    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest=\"command\", required=True)\n    serve = sub.add_parser(\"serve\", help=\"run the admin console locally\")\n    serve.add_argument(\"--port\", type=int, default=8000)\n    serve.add_argument(\"--reload\", action=\"store_true\", default=True)\n    sub.add_parser(\"publish\", help=\"render site/ from published content\")\n    deploy = sub.add_parser(\"deploy\", help=\"push site/ to gh-pages\")\n    deploy.add_argument(\"--message\", default=\"Publish site\")\n    create = sub.add_parser(\"create-admin\", help=\"create an Admin account\")\n    create.add_argument(\"email\")\n    create.add_argument(\"--name\", default=\"Admin\", help=\"display name\")\n    args = parser.parse_args(argv)\n\n    if args.command == \"create-admin\":\n        password = getpass.getpass(\"Password: \")\n        if password != getpass.getpass(\"Password again: \"):\n            print(\"The passwords did not match; nothing was created.\")\n            return 1\n        users.init_db(settings.DATABASE_PATH)\n        try:\n            users.create_user(settings.DATABASE_PATH, email=args.email,\n                              password=password, role=\"admin\",\n                              display_name=args.name)\n        except sqlite3.IntegrityError:\n            print(f\"{args.email} already has an account.\")\n            return 1\n        print(f\"Created Admin {args.email}.\")\n        return 0\n\n    if args.command == \"serve\":\n        if settings.SECRET_KEY == settings.DEV_SECRET_KEY:\n            print(\"CMS_SECRET_KEY is not set (copy .env.example to .env and \"\n                  \"change it). Refusing to serve with the public dev key.\")\n            return 1\n        import uvicorn\n\n        uvicorn.run(\"app.main:app\", port=args.port, reload=args.reload)\n        return 0\n\n    if args.command == \"publish\":\n        try:\n            out, items = render()\n        except DraftInOutput as exc:\n            print(exc)\n            return 1\n        print(\"Rendered:\")\n        for item in items:\n            print(f\"  - {item}\")\n        print(f\"Wrote {out}. Preview it with:  python3 -m http.server -d {out} 8001\")\n        return 0\n\n    if args.command == \"deploy\":\n        if not settings.SITE.exists():\n            print(\"site/ does not exist yet — run `uv run cms publish` first.\")\n            return 1\n        result = subprocess.run(\n            [sys.executable, \"-m\", \"ghp_import\", \"-n\", \"-p\", \"-m\", args.message,\n             str(settings.SITE)],\n        )\n        if result.returncode == 0:\n            print(\"Pushed to gh-pages. Settings -> Pages -> Deploy from a branch -> \"\n                  \"gh-pages / root, then wait up to 10 minutes.\")\n        return result.returncode\n    return 1\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    raise SystemExit(main())\n\"\"\"Render the public site into site/ as plain HTML.\n\nT00 publishes a placeholder home page. As you build content types, extend\nrender_site() to write one file per published item. Two rules the rubric checks:\n\n  1. Only PUBLISHED content is written here. A draft that reaches site/ is a bug.\n  2. Every href and src is RELATIVE (\"style.css\", \"posts/x.html\"), never\n     root-absolute (\"/style.css\"), because Pages serves this from a subfolder.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport re\nimport shutil\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom markupsafe import escape\nfrom jinja2 import Environment, FileSystemLoader, select_autoescape\n\nfrom app import settings\nfrom app.markdown import render_markdown\nfrom app.posts import format_byline\nfrom app.users import connect\n\nCSS = \"\"\"/* Minimal starter styles — make them yours. */\n:root { color-scheme: light dark; }\nbody { font: 16px/1.6 system-ui, sans-serif; margin: 0 auto; max-width: 42rem; padding: 1rem; }\nheader a { font-weight: 700; text-decoration: none; }\nmain { margin-block: 2rem; }\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef environment() -> Environment:\n    return Environment(\n        loader=FileSystemLoader(str(settings.TEMPLATES)),\n        autoescape=select_autoescape([\"html\"]),\n    )\n\n\nclass DraftInOutput(Exception):\n    \"\"\"A Draft's title or Slug would appear in the generated site.\"\"\"\n\n\ndef _posts_where(database: Path, where: str) -> list:\n    if not database.exists():\n        return []\n    with connect(database) as conn:\n        if not conn.execute(\"select 1 from sqlite_master where name='posts'\").fetchone():\n            return []\n        return conn.execute(\n            f\"select * from posts where {where} order by published_at desc, id desc\"\n        ).fetchall()\n\n\ndef _published_posts(database: Path) -> list:\n    return _posts_where(database, \"status='published'\")\n\n\ndef _draft_rows(database: Path) -> list:\n    return _posts_where(database, \"status!='published'\")\n\n\ndef _first_paragraph(html: str) -> str:\n    match = re.search(r\"<p>.*?</p>\", html, re.S)\n    return match.group(0) if match else \"\"\n\n\ndef _check_no_drafts(site: Path, database: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Refuse if any Draft made it into any page of the site.\n\n    Every post entry carries a data-post-id marker and links to\n    posts/<slug>.html. Both are compared with the Drafts straight from the\n    database (ids and Slugs are unique), on every file, so a Draft leaking onto\n    Home or News is caught as surely as one on its own page. Titles are not\n    matched: a Draft called \"News\" must not block every publish.\n    \"\"\"\n    drafts = _draft_rows(database)\n    for f in sorted(site.rglob(\"*\")):\n        if not f.is_file():\n            continue\n        text = f.read_text()\n        marked = {int(i) for i in re.findall(r'data-post-id=\"(\\d+)\"', text)}\n        for draft in drafts:\n            if (draft[\"id\"] in marked or f\"posts/{draft['slug']}.html\" in text\n                    or f.name == f\"{draft['slug']}.html\" and f.parent.name == \"posts\"):\n                raise DraftInOutput(\n                    f\"Refusing to publish: the Draft \\\"{draft['title']}\\\" would \"\n                    f\"appear in {f.relative_to(site)}. Drafts must never leave the \"\n                    \"database. The site was not written.\")\n\n\ndef render(out: Path | None = None, database: Path | None = None) -> tuple[Path, list[str]]:\n    \"\"\"Render Published content into `out`; return it and a list of rendered items.\n\n    Builds in a sibling folder and swaps it in only after the Draft check\n    passes, so a refusal never leaves a half-finished site.\n    \"\"\"\n    out = out or settings.SITE\n    database = database or settings.DATABASE_PATH\n    work = out.parent / (out.name + \".building\")\n    if work.exists():\n        shutil.rmtree(work)\n    work.mkdir(parents=True)\n    try:\n        items = _render_into(work, database)\n    except BaseException:\n        shutil.rmtree(work, ignore_errors=True)\n        raise\n    if out.exists():\n        shutil.rmtree(out)\n    work.rename(out)\n    return out, items\n\n\ndef _render_into(work: Path, database: Path) -> list[str]:\n    env = environment()\n    published = _published_posts(database)\n    entries = []\n    for post in published:\n        body = render_markdown(post[\"body\"])\n        entries.append({\n            \"id\": post[\"id\"], \"title\": post[\"title\"], \"slug\": post[\"slug\"], \"body\": body,\n            \"excerpt\": _first_paragraph(body),\n            \"date\": (post[\"published_at\"] or \"\")[:10],\n            \"byline\": format_byline(post[\"byline_name\"], post[\"byline_title\"],\n                                    post[\"byline_term\"]),\n            \"href\": f\"posts/{post['slug']}.html\",\n        })\n\n    (work / \"style.css\").write_text(CSS)\n    (work / \"posts\").mkdir()\n    nav = {\"home_path\": \"index.html\", \"news_path\": \"news.html\"}\n\n    def write(path: str, template: str, prefix: str, **ctx) -> None:\n        # prefix is \"\" for top-level pages and \"../\" for pages in posts/\n        (work / path).write_text(env.get_template(template).render(\n            title=settings.SITE_TITLE, css_path=prefix + \"style.css\",\n            home_path=prefix + nav[\"home_path\"], news_path=prefix + nav[\"news_path\"],\n            **ctx))\n\n    write(\"index.html\", \"public/home.html\", \"\", items=entries[:5])\n    write(\"news.html\", \"public/news.html\", \"\", items=entries)\n    for e in entries:\n        write(e[\"href\"], \"public/post.html\", \"../\", post=e)\n    _check_no_drafts(work, database)\n    return [\"Home (index.html)\", \"News (news.html)\"] + [\n        f\"Post: {e['title']} ({e['href']})\" for e in entries]\n\n\ndef render_site(out: Path | None = None, database: Path | None = None) -> token field\"\n    return match.group(1)\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef db_path(tmp_path):\n    path = tmp_path / \"test.db\"\n    users.init_db(path)\n    for role, info in DEMO_USERS.items():\n        users.create_user(path, email=info[\"email\"], password=info[\"password\"],\n                          role=role, display_name=info[\"display_name\"])\n    return path\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef make_client(db_path):\n    \"\"\"Build a fresh, anonymous client against the temporary database.\"\"\"\n\n    def _make() -> TestClient:\n        return TestClient(create_app(database=db_path))\n\n    return _make\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef client(make_client) -> TestClient:\n    return make_client()\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef client_as(make_client):\n    \"\"\"Return a factory: client_as(\"editor\") -> a logged-in TestClient.\"\"\"\n\n    def _login(role: str) -> TestClient:\n        user = DEMO_USERS[role]\n        c = make_client()\n        form = c.get(\"/login\")\n        if form.status_code == 404:\n            pytest.skip(\"No /login route yet — build the login ticket first.\")\n        response = c.post(\"/login\", data={\"email\": user[\"email\"],\n
